 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>FIBERNET TELECOM GROUP, INC. </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"
ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS&#151;(Continued) </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In addition, the Plan provides that if the benefits payable in connection with a Change in Control
event are subject to an Excise Tax (as defined in the Plan), then (i)&nbsp;for the President, FiberNet agrees to make such individual whole by making a gross-up payment, such that the net total amount of benefits actually received is equal to those
benefits calculated as if only standard income taxes, and not the Excise Tax, had applied, and (ii)&nbsp;for each Vice President and Senior Vice President, FiberNet shall pay to such individual the greater of (A)&nbsp;the total value of the benefits
payable if reduced to avoid triggering the Excise Tax or (B)&nbsp;the total value of the benefits payable even if the Excise Tax applies, such that the net total amount of benefits actually received by such individual is maximized. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company and its subsidiaries, in the normal course of business, may become party to any number of judicial, regulatory and administrative
proceedings. The Company&#146;s management does not believe that any material liability will be imposed as a result of any of these matters. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>9. STOCK
RESERVED FOR FUTURE ISSUANCE </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In addition to shares of common stock underlying outstanding stock options, the Company has reserved for
future issuance of additional shares of common stock relating to outstanding warrants. As of December&nbsp;31, 2007, the Company had outstanding warrants exercisable into approximately 0.8&nbsp;million shares of common stock at exercise prices
ranging from $0.10 to $36.00 per share. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>10. RELATED PARTY TRANSACTIONS </B></FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">As a result amendments to the Former Facility in 2003, the Company&#146;s Lenders became related parties. Deutsche Bank Trust Company North America, as
subsidiary of Deutsche Bank AG, is the Company&#146;s primary bank, where it maintains its cash accounts. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">On February&nbsp;1, 2005, the
Company entered into a consulting agreement with Michael Liss, the Chairman of the Board of Directors and former President and Chief Executive Officer. Pursuant to the terms of this agreement, Mr.&nbsp;Liss provided general financial advisory
services, including assisting the Company in analyzing and evaluating potential strategic transactions and public or private placements of debt or equity securities. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, Mr.&nbsp;Liss received $0.2 million as
compensation for his services, including reimbursement for the payment of health, life and disability insurance premiums and for travel expenses incurred in connection with the performance of his agreement, as well as customary indemnification. The
consulting agreement was terminated on October&nbsp;31, 2005. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>11. INCOME TAXES </B></FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company accounts for income taxes in accordance with the provisions of SFAS No.&nbsp;109. During 2007, 2006 and 2005 the Company did not record a
current or deferred federal income tax provision because the Company had losses for federal income tax purposes. The Company has recorded a full valuation allowance, as it does not expect to realize the benefits of the net operating tax losses
generated in those years. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">58 </FONT></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The temporary differences described above represent differences between the tax basis of assets or
liabilities and their reported amounts in the consolidated financial statements that will result in taxable or deductible amounts in future years when the reported amounts of the assets or liabilities are recovered or settled. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">During the tax years ended December&nbsp;31, 2007 and 2006, a valuation allowance for the full amount of the net deferred tax asset was recorded. The
valuation allowance represents the portion of tax net operating loss carryforwards and other items for which it is more likely than not that the benefit of such items will not be realized. Based upon the level of historical losses and projections
for the future taxable periods, it is more likely than not that the Company will not realize the benefit of the deferred tax asset. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">As of
December&nbsp;31, 2007, the Company had net operating loss carryforwards for federal income tax purposes of approximately $206 million substantially all of which are subject to a de minimus annual limitation on future utilization under
Section&nbsp;382 of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986. These tax loss carryforwards begin expiring in 2012. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Section&nbsp;382 imposes
limitations on the availability of a Company&#146;s net operating losses after a more than 50 percentage point ownership change occurs. The Company believes that the most recent ownership changes </FONT>
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>

<TD WIDTH="3%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="1">(a)</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="1">Includes a $ 1.1million loss on early extinguishment of debt </FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>13.
ACQUISITION OF GATEWAY COLOCATION </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">On January&nbsp;30, 2004, the Company completed the acquisition of the operating assets of
gateway.realty.new jersey.llc, also known as Gateway Colocation. The transaction was structured as an acquisition of assets, and the purchase agreement included an &#147;earn-out&#148; payment based upon the achievement of certain financial
objectives with respect to the revenues generated by the facility, which if payable, will be paid in shares of the Company&#146;s common stock. On March&nbsp;22, 2006, the Company entered into an amendment to the purchase agreement. The terms of the
amendment extend the duration of the &#147;earn-out&#148; provision for an additional twenty-four months retroactive to January&nbsp;30, 2006(see subsequent event- Note 15). </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>14. TERMINATION OF CONSOLIDATED EDISON COMMUNICATIONS STOCK PURCHASE AGREEMENT </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">On December&nbsp;10,
2004, the Company entered into a stock purchase agreement with Consolidated Edison, Inc. (the &#147;Con Ed Agreement&#148;) for the purchase of all of the issued and outstanding shares of Consolidated Edison Communications Holding Company, Inc.
(&#147;CEC&#148;), a wholly-owned subsidiary. On May&nbsp;4, 2005, the Company entered into a Mutual Termination and Release Agreement (the &#147;Termination Agreement&#148;), with Consolidated Edison, Inc., which terminated the Con Ed Agreement.
The Con Ed Agreement was terminated because of the parties&#146; inability to resolve certain contractual issues. The Termination Agreement contains mutual releases of liability. The Company did not incur any early termination penalties in
connection with the termination of the Con Ed Agreement. As a result of the termination of the Con Ed Agreement the Company included a total of $1.1 million of transaction costs in selling, general and administrative expenses for the year ended
December&nbsp;31, 2005. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">60 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>15. SUBSEQUENT EVENTS </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; text-indent:4%"><FONT
FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">On January&nbsp;31, 2008, the Company received written notice from Gateway Colocation pursuant to the terms of the &#147;earn out&#148; provision acknowledging that the financial objectives had been achieved and, in

<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">(a)</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Evaluation of Disclosure Controls and Procedures </FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our
management, with the participation of our chief executive officer and chief financial officer, evaluated the effectiveness of our disclosure controls and procedures as of December&nbsp;31, 2007. The term &#147;disclosure controls and
procedures,&#148; as defined in Rules 13a-15(e) and 15d-15(e) under the Securities and Exchange Act of 1934, means controls and other procedures of a company that are designed to ensure that information required to be disclosed by a company in the
reports that it files or submits under the Exchange Act is recorded, processed, summarized, and reported, within the time periods specified in the SEC&#146;s rules and forms. Disclosure controls and procedures include, without limitation, controls
and procedures designed to ensure that information required to be disclosed by a company in the reports that it files and submits under the Exchange Act is accumulated and communicated to the company&#146;s management, including its principal
executive and principal financial officers, as appropriate to allow timely decisions regarding required disclosure. Based on the evaluation of our disclosure controls and procedures as of December&nbsp;31, 2007, our chief executive officer and chief
financial officer concluded that, as of such date, our disclosure controls and procedures were effective. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Audit Committee of the Board
of Directors, which is composed solely of independent directors, meets regularly with our independent registered public accounting firm, Friedman LLP, and representatives of management to review accounting, financial reporting, internal control and
audit matters, as well as the nature and extent of the audit effort. The Audit Committee is responsible for the engagement of the independent auditors. The independent auditors have free access to the Audit Committee. </FONT></P> <P
STYLE="font-size:12px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

represent the Company&#146;s consolidated financial position and results of operations for the periods and as of the dates stated therein. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Management&#146;s Assessment of Internal Control Over Financial Reporting </FONT></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT
FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The management of FiberNet Telecom Group, Inc. is responsible for establishing and maintaining adequate internal control over financial reporting as defined by Rules 13a&#150;15(f) and 15(d)-15(f) under the Securities
and Exchange Act of 1934.&nbsp;This system is designed to provide reasonable assurance regarding the reliability of financial reporting and the preparation of financial statements for external purposes in accordance with accounting principals
generally accepted in the United States of America. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our internal control over financial reporting includes those policies and procedures
that: (1)&nbsp;pertain to the maintenance of records that, in reasonable detail, accurately and fairly reflect the transactions and dispositions of the assets of the Company; (2)&nbsp;provide reasonable assurance that transactions are recorded as
necessary to permit preparation of financial statements in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles, and that receipts and expenditures of the Company are being made only in accordance with authorizations of management and directors
of the </FONT>
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">62 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%">
<FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Company; and (3)&nbsp;provide reasonable assurance regarding prevention or timely detection of unauthorized acquisition, use or disposition of the
Company&#146;s assets that could have a material effect on the financial statements. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Because of its inherent limitations, a system of
internal control over financial reporting can only provide reasonable assurance and may not prevent or detect misstatements.&nbsp;Further, because of changes in conditions, effectiveness of internal control over financial reporting may vary over
time. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:8%"><FONT FACE="Times New Roman" SIZE="2">With the participation of our chief executive officer and chief financial officer, management conducted an evaluation of the
effectiveness of the system of internal control over financial reporting based on the framework in <I>Internal Control-Integrated Framework</I>, published by the Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of the Treadway Commission (COSO).&nbsp;Based on
this evaluation, management determined that the Company&#146;s system of internal control over financial reporting was effective as of December&nbsp;31, 2007. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
